after CP invariance is incorporated. We only show the results for NO neutrino masses, similar results can be obtained for IO.

Furthermore, we notice that the predictions for the CP phases
\delta_{CP}
,
\alpha_{21}
and
\alpha_{31}
(
\phi
for
m_{1}=0
) scatter in wide ranges. The future long baseline neutrino experiments DUNE and Hyper-Kamiokande, if running in both neutrino and anti-neutrino modes, will allow for a measurement of the Dirac CP phase with a certain precision and have thus the potential to rule out some of our model. The Majorana phase could be probed or at least constrained by neutrinoless double beta decay (
0\nu\beta\beta
) experiments. The dependence of the
0\nu\beta\beta
decay amplitude on the mixing parameters enter through the effective Majorana neutrino mass
m_{\beta\beta}
with

m_{\beta\beta}=\left|m_{1}\cos^{2}\theta_{12}\cos^{2}\theta_{13}+m_{2}\sin^{2}\theta_{12}\cos^{2}\theta_{13}e^{i\alpha_{21}}+m_{3}\sin^{2}\theta_{13}e^{i\left(\alpha_{31}-2\delta_{CP}\right)}\right|\,,

(67)

which involves all mixing parameters except
\theta_{23}
. If the lightest neutrino is massless,
m_{\beta\beta}
takes a simpler form,

m_{\beta\beta}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lc}\left|m_{2}\sin^{2}\theta_{12}\cos^{2}\theta_{13}e^{i\phi}+m_{3}\sin^{2}\theta_{13}e^{-i2\delta_{CP}}\right|,&~m_{1}=0\,,\\[7.22743pt]
\left|m_{1}\cos^{2}\theta_{12}\cos^{2}\theta_{13}+m_{2}\sin^{2}\theta_{12}\cos^{2}\theta_{13}e^{i\phi}\right|,&~m_{3}=0\,.\end{array}\right.

(68)

The most stringent bound on the effective Majorana neutrino mass is
m_{\beta\beta}<(61\sim 165)
meV from KamLAND-Zen
[
75
]
. We see that these viable models predict
m_{\beta\beta}<80
meV except the model
\mathcal{L}_{654}
which gives
m_{\beta\beta}=153.1525
meV. All these values of the effective Majorana neutrino mass
m_{\beta\beta}
are below the upper bound of KamLAND-Zen. In the two right-handed neutrino models,
m_{\beta\beta}
is determined to be few meV and it is far below the sensitivity of future
0\nu\beta\beta
experiments. It is well-known that neutrino masses can be directly probed by kinematic studies of weak-interaction processes such as
\beta
decay of tritium, and the effective neutrino mass measured in beta decay is

\displaystyle m_{\beta}=\sqrt{m^{2}_{1}\cos^{2}\theta_{12}\cos^{2}\theta_{13}+m^{2}_{2}\sin^{2}\theta_{12}\cos^{2}\theta_{13}+m^{2}_{3}\sin^{2}\theta_{13}}\,,

(69)

which is independent of CP phases. The latest bound on
m_{\beta}
is
m_{\beta}<1.1
eV at
90\%
confidence level from KATRIN
[
76
]
, and the sensitivity on
m_{\beta}
is expected to be improved by one order of magnitude down to
0.2
eV. We see that the KATRIN bound
m_{\beta}<1.1
eV is safely fulfilled in all these 25 models.

4.4
Lepton models with gCP

As shown in section
3
, the generalized CP symmetry enforces all coupling constants to be real in our working basis. In other words, CP invariance requires the phases of coupling constants are equal to
0
or
\pi
. Thus the VEV of complex modulus
\tau
would be the unique
source of all CP violating phases. As a consequence, the free parameters of the
25
lepton models in table
4
are reduced by two after incorporating CP invariance. We find that only 6 models are excluded by the experimental data after gCP symmetry is imposed, and the remaining 19 models were still in good agreement with the experiments. The minimal models with gCP and
A^{\prime}_{5}
modular symmetry are
\mathcal{L}_{22},\,\mathcal{L}_{66},\,\mathcal{L}_{94},\,\mathcal{L}_{95},\,\mathcal{L}_{99},\,\mathcal{L}_{100},\,\mathcal{L}_{108},\,\mathcal{L}_{113}
and
\mathcal{L}_{118}
which use 7 real free parameters to describe the 12 observables including 3 charged lepton masses, 3 light neutrino masses, 3 lepton mixing angles and 3 CP violation phases. The right-handed charged leptons transform as the direct sum of doublet and singlet of
A^{\prime}_{5}
in all these minimal models. The numerical results are summarized in table
8
and table
9
, we see that the current bounds on both
m_{\beta}
and
m_{\beta\beta}
are satisfied as well.

In the models
\mathcal{L}_{66}
,
\mathcal{L}_{108}
,
\mathcal{L}_{113}
and
\mathcal{L}_{118}
, the charged lepton mass matrix depends on four coupling constants
\alpha
,
\beta
,
\gamma
and
\delta
while the light neutrino mass matrix only depends on the modulus
\tau
up to the overall scale
g^{2}v^{2}_{u}/\Lambda
such that the neutrino mass ratios are completely determined by the value of
\tau
. For the models
\mathcal{L}_{22}
,
\mathcal{L}_{94}
,
\mathcal{L}_{95}
,
\mathcal{L}_{99}
and
\mathcal{L}_{100}
, the charged lepton mass hierarchies rely on cancellation of the comparable
\alpha
and
\beta
terms, this is a new feature in comparison with other modular models in the literature. It is notable that the VEVs of
\tau
are very close to the CP conserved points
\texttt{Re}(\tau)=\pm 1/2
in these models, and departure from
\texttt{Re}(\tau)=\pm 1/2
leads to nontrivial CP violating phases. As regards the description of charged lepton masses, the model
\mathcal{L}_{22}
is superior to the others, since the best fit value of the input parameter
\beta/\alpha=-1.4038
is of order one. Moreover, the model
\mathcal{L}_{22}
predicts the neutrino mass sum
\sum^{3}_{i=1}m_{i}=120.9755~\text{meV}
and the Dirac CP phase
\delta_{CP}
around
1.5\pi
. These predictions can be tested in future neutrino experiments.

In the following, we take the model
\mathcal{L}_{22}
as an example for illustration. The charged lepton mass term depends on three couplings
\alpha
,
\beta
and
\gamma
while the neutrino superpotential depends on two coupling constants
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
besides the flavor scale
\Lambda
. Moreover,
\alpha
,
\gamma
and
g_{1}
can be taken real by field redefinition while the parameters
\beta
and
g_{2}
are generically complex parameters without gCP and they become real once gCP is imposed. We use the parameter scan tool
MultiNest

[
77
,
78
]
to efficiently explore the parameter space, and we use a
\chi^{2}
function defined as usual to serve as a test-statistic for the goodness-of-fit. The charged lepton masses, neutrino mass squared differences and the neutrino mixing angles are required to lie in
3\sigma
regions. The correlations among input parameters and observables are plotted in figure
1
, where the green points and red points are for the scenarios without gCP and with gCP respectively. Obviously the allowed regions of the input parameters are reduced considerably after considering gCP, and accordingly the predictions for observables shrink to quite small regions.

Figure 1:
The predicted correlations among the input free parameters, neutrino mixing angles and CP violating phases in the lepton model
\mathcal{L}_{22}
with (red) and without (green) gCP symmetry.

5
Unified description of all fermions

As shown in previous section, one can construct predictive lepton models based on the homogeneous finite modular group
A^{\prime}_{5}
. In this section, we shall apply
A^{\prime}_{5}
to explain the quark masses and CKM mixing matrix. Analogous to what we have done for charged lepton sector in section
4.1
, we can systematically analyze the possible quark models with
A^{\prime}_{5}
modular symmetry. We find that the measured quark masses and CKM mixing matrix can be explained in terms of a few free parameters if quark fields are embedded in doublet and singlet representations of
A^{\prime}_{5}
. We have made a systematic classification of quark models for this kind of quark arrangement and generalize the strategy
of numerical analysis for leptons to the quark sector. The values of quark masses and CKM parameters are taken from
[
74
]
,

\displaystyle m_{u}/m_{c}=(1.9286\pm 0.6017)\times 10^{-3}\,,\quad m_{c}/m_{t}=(2.8213\pm 0.1195)\times 10^{-3}\,,

\displaystyle m_{d}/m_{s}=(5.0523\pm 0.6191)\times 10^{-2}\,,\quad m_{s}/m_{b}=(1.8241\pm 0.1005)\times 10^{-2}\,,

\displaystyle m_{t}=87.4555\mathrm{GeV}\,,\quad m_{b}=0.9682\mathrm{GeV}\,,\quad\delta_{CP}^{q}/\pi=0.3845\pm 0.0173\,,

\displaystyle\theta_{12}^{q}=0.22736\pm 0.00073\,,\quad\theta_{13}^{q}=0.00349\pm 0.00013\,,\quad\theta_{23}^{q}=0.04015\pm 0.00064\,,

(70)

which are calculated at the GUT scale with
\tan\beta=10
and the SUSY breaking scale
M_{\text{SUSY}}=10
TeV. In the end, we find four models out of 892 possibilities can describe the experimental data of quarks with 11 free parameters including
\texttt{Re}(\tau)
and
\texttt{Im}(\tau)
.
A systematic classification of the quark models as well as the numerical results are included in the supplementary file
[
72
]
.

Furthermore, we combine the
25
viable lepton models collected in table
4
with the two candidate quark models to give a unified description of quark and lepton in a single model. The crucial point is whether there exist common values of
\tau
for which the experimental data of both quarks and leptons can be reproduced. It is remarkable that we really find a quark-lepton unification model: the lepton sector is the model
\mathcal{L}_{2}
and the quark sector is
\mathcal{Q}_{3}
which can be found in the supplementary material
[
72
]
. In the model
\mathcal{Q}_{3}
, the quark fields and the Higgs fields transform under modular symmetry as follows,

\displaystyle\rho_{Q}=\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{1}\,,\quad\rho_{U^{c}}=\rho_{D^{c}}=\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{1}\,,\quad\rho_{H_{u}}=\rho_{H_{d}}=\mathbf{1}\,,

\displaystyle k_{Q_{3}}=k_{Q_{D}}-5=-k_{U^{c}_{D}}-3=-k_{U^{c}_{3}}=-k_{D^{c}_{D}}-1=-k_{D^{c}_{3}}\,,\quad k_{H_{u}}=k_{H_{d}}=0\,,

(71)

where we denote
Q_{D}\equiv(Q_{1},~Q_{2})^{T},\,U^{c}_{D}\equiv(U_{1}^{c},~U_{2}^{c})^{T},\,D^{c}_{D}\equiv(D^{c}_{1},~D^{c}_{2})^{T}
, and the modular weight
k_{Q}
is a general integer. Thus the modular invariant superpotentials of quark sector are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{u}=\alpha_{u}\left(Y_{\mathbf{3}^{\prime}}^{(2)}U^{c}_{D}Q_{D}H_{u}\right)_{\mathbf{1}}+\beta_{u}\left(Y_{\mathbf{2}^{\prime}}^{(5)}U^{c}_{3}Q_{D}H_{u}\right)_{\mathbf{1}}+\gamma_{u}\left(U^{c}_{3}Q_{3}H_{u}\right)_{\mathbf{1}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{d}=\alpha_{d}\left(Y_{\mathbf{1}}^{(4)}D^{c}_{D}Q_{D}H_{d}\right)_{\mathbf{1}}+\beta_{d}\left(Y_{\mathbf{3}^{\prime}}^{(4)}D^{c}_{D}Q_{D}H_{d}\right)_{\mathbf{1}}+\gamma_{d}\left(Y_{\mathbf{2}^{\prime}}^{(5)}D^{c}_{3}Q_{D}H_{d}\right)_{\mathbf{1}}+\delta_{d}\left(D^{c}_{3}Q_{3}H_{d}\right)_{\mathbf{1}}\,.

(72)

The phases of
\alpha_{u}
,
\beta_{u}
,
\gamma_{u}
,
\alpha_{d}
and
\gamma_{d}
are unphysical and they can be absorbed by quark fields, nevertheless the phases of
\beta_{d}
and
\delta_{d}
can not be removed by field redefinition. The corresponding up and down quark masses matrices can be written as

\displaystyle M_{u}=\begin{pmatrix}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{u}Y_{\mathbf{3}^{\prime},3}^{(2)}&\alpha_{u}Y_{\mathbf{3}^{\prime},1}^{(2)}&0\\
\alpha_{u}Y_{\mathbf{3}^{\prime},1}^{(2)}&\sqrt{2}\alpha_{u}Y_{\mathbf{3}^{\prime},2}^{(2)}&0\\
-\beta_{u}Y_{\mathbf{2}^{\prime},2}^{(5)}&\beta_{u}Y_{\mathbf{2}^{\prime},1}^{(5)}&\gamma_{u}\\
\end{pmatrix}v_{u}\,,~M_{d}=\begin{pmatrix}-\sqrt{2}\beta_{d}Y_{\mathbf{3}^{\prime},3}^{(4)}&\beta_{d}Y_{\mathbf{3}^{\prime},1}^{(4)}-\alpha_{d}Y_{\mathbf{1},1}^{(4)}&0\\
\alpha_{d}Y_{\mathbf{1},1}^{(4)}+\beta_{d}Y_{\mathbf{3}^{\prime},1}^{(4)}&\sqrt{2}\beta_{d}Y_{\mathbf{3}^{\prime},2}^{(4)}&0\\
-\gamma_{d}Y_{\mathbf{2}^{\prime},2}^{(5)}&\gamma_{d}Y_{\mathbf{2}^{\prime},1}^{(5)}&\delta_{d}\\
\end{pmatrix}v_{d}\,.

(73)

Notice that both (13) and (23) entries of
M_{u}
and
M_{d}
vanish exactly. This model uses 15 real dimensionless parameters to describe the mixing angles, CP violation phases and the masses ratios of both quarks and leptons. The 4 overall scale factors
\alpha v_{d}
,
g_{1}v_{u}^{2}/\Lambda
,
\alpha_{u}v_{u}
and
\alpha_{d}v_{d}
give the absolute masses scale of the charged leptons, neutrinos, up quarks and down quarks. By scanning the parameter space of the complete model, we identify the best fit point of the input parameters,

\displaystyle\langle\tau\rangle=-0.499996+0.894402i\,,~~\beta/\alpha=24.5400\,,~~\gamma/\alpha=654.9320\,,~~\delta/\alpha=100.8429e^{4.3652i}\,,

\displaystyle g_{2}/g_{1}=0.3818e^{5.5110i}\,,~~\beta_{u}/\alpha_{u}=32.6296\,,~~\gamma_{u}/\alpha_{u}=11.1981\,,~~\beta_{d}/\alpha_{d}=6.3229e^{0.00068i}\,,

\displaystyle\gamma_{d}/\alpha_{d}=1.7462\,,~~\delta_{d}/\alpha_{d}=1.9895e^{5.4463i}\,,~~\alpha v_{d}=0.04361~\text{MeV}\,,~~g_{1}v_{u}^{2}/\Lambda=1.0895~\text{meV}\,,

\displaystyle\alpha_{u}v_{u}=0.03432~\text{GeV}\,,\quad\alpha_{d}v_{d}=0.00243~\text{GeV}\,.

(74)

The corresponding predictions for masses and mixing parameters of quarks and leptons are determined to be

\displaystyle\sin^{2}\theta^{l}_{13}=0.02231\,,\quad\sin^{2}\theta^{l}_{12}=0.3019\,,\quad\sin^{2}\theta^{l}_{23}=0.4570\,,\quad\delta^{l}_{CP}=1.1671\pi\,,

\displaystyle\alpha_{21}=1.3593\pi\,,\quad\alpha_{31}=0.4762\pi\,,\quad m_{e}/m_{\mu}=0.00474\,,\quad m_{\mu}/m_{\tau}=0.05857\,,

\displaystyle m_{1}=77.2709~\text{meV}\,,\quad m_{2}=77.7495~\text{meV}\,,\quad m_{3}=92.1717~\text{meV}\,,

\displaystyle\sum_{i}m_{i}=247.1921~\text{meV}\,,\quad m_{\beta}=77.7755~\text{meV}\,,\quad m_{\beta\beta}=48.9783~\text{meV}\,,

\displaystyle\theta^{q}_{13}=0.003498\,,\quad\theta^{q}_{12}=0.22764\,,\quad\theta^{q}_{23}=0.04023\,,\quad\delta^{q}_{CP}=69.1740^{\circ}\,,

\displaystyle m_{u}/m_{c}=0.00021\,,\quad m_{c}/m_{t}=0.00282\,,\quad m_{d}/m_{s}=0.03457\,,\quad m_{s}/m_{b}=0.01769\,,

(75)

which are in the experimentally preferred
3\sigma
ranges. It is interesting to note that the common value of
\tau
is very close to the fixed point
\tau_{ST}=e^{2\pi i/3}
which preserves a
Z^{ST}_{3}
residual symmetry.

6
Extension to rational weight modular forms at level 5

The modular weights could be rational numbers, as shown in the string construction
[
79
,
80
,
11
]
. It is interesting to study the fractional weight modular forms from the bottom-up modular invariance approach. To discuss rational weight modular forms, it is convenient to consider the metaplectic cover of the modular group
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})

[
58
]
. For the concerned case, we should consider the
5-fold covering of
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
and it is denoted as
\widetilde{\Gamma}
with

\widetilde{\Gamma}=\Big\{\widetilde{\gamma}=(\gamma,\phi(\gamma,\tau))~|~\gamma\in\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in SL(2,\mathbb{Z}),\quad\phi(\gamma,\tau)^{5}=(c\tau+d)\Big\}\,.

(76)

The action of
\widetilde{\gamma}
on
\tau
is the same as that of
\gamma
, i.e.
\widetilde{\gamma}\tau=\gamma\tau
. The group multiplication of
\widetilde{\Gamma}
is defined as

(\gamma_{1},\phi_{1}(\gamma_{1},\tau))(\gamma_{2},\phi_{2}(\gamma_{2},\tau))=(\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2},\phi_{1}(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\tau)\phi_{2}(\gamma_{2},\tau))\,,

(77)

where
\phi_{1}=\epsilon_{1}(c\tau+d)^{1/5}
,
\phi_{2}=\epsilon_{2}(c\tau+d)^{1/5}
and
\epsilon_{1,2}\in\{1,\omega_{5},\omega_{5}^{2},\omega_{5}^{3},\omega_{5}^{4}\}
. The principal branch of the quintic root is chosen here. Obviously each element
\gamma\in SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
corresponds to five element
\tilde{\gamma}=(\gamma,\omega^{j}_{5}(c\tau+d)^{1/5})
of the metaplectic group
\widetilde{\Gamma}
with
\omega_{5}=e^{2\pi i/5}
and
j=0,1,2,3,4
.
The group
\widetilde{\Gamma}
can be generated by

\widetilde{S}=\left(\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}\,,~\omega_{5}^{2}(-\tau)^{1/5}\right)\,,\quad\widetilde{T}=\left(\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\,,1\right)\,,

(78)

where

(-\tau)^{1/5}
denotes the principal branch of quintic root, possessing positive real part. It follows that

\widetilde{S}^{2}=\widetilde{R}\,,\quad\widetilde{R}^{10}=1\,,\quad(\widetilde{S}\widetilde{T})^{3}=1\,,

(79)

with

\widetilde{R}=\left(\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix}\,,-\omega_{5}\right)\,.

(80)

Therefore the elements
\widetilde{S}
and
\widetilde{S}\widetilde{T}
are of orders 20 and 3 respectively while
\widetilde{T}
is of infinite order. Obviously the element
\widetilde{R}
generates the center of
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
.
The metaplectic principal congruence subgroup at level 5 is defined as

\widetilde{\Gamma}(5)=\Big\{\widetilde{h}=(h,v_{5}(h)J_{1/5}(h,\tau))\Big|h\in\Gamma(5)\Big\}\,,

(81)

where
J_{1/5}(h,\tau)=(c\tau+d)^{1/5}
,
v_{5}(h)
is the multiplier of the weight
1/5
modular forms
3
3

3

The weight
1/5
modular forms for
\Gamma(5)
are holomorphic functions satisfying
f(\gamma\tau)=v_{5}(\gamma)(c\tau+d)^{1/5}f(\tau)
for
\gamma\in\Gamma(5)
.
for
\Gamma(5)
, and the identity
v^{5}_{5}(h)=1
is fulfilled for all
h\in\Gamma(5)
. The explicit expression of
v_{5}(h)
has been given in
[
68
,
58
]
, it is too lengthy to present here. It is obvious that
\widetilde{\Gamma}(5)
is isomorphic to
\Gamma(5)
, and it is a normal subgroup of
\widetilde{\Gamma}
. In particular, the element
\widetilde{T}^{5}
belongs to
\widetilde{\Gamma}(5)
. Taking the group quotient, we can obtain the finite metaplectic group
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}=

\widetilde{\Gamma}/\Gamma(5)\cong A^{\prime}_{5}\times Z_{5}
which can be obtained by imposing another condition
\widetilde{T}^{5}=1
. Thus the generator relations of
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
are

\widetilde{S}^{2}=\widetilde{R}\,,\quad\widetilde{R}^{10}=1\,,\quad(\widetilde{S}\widetilde{T})^{3}=\widetilde{T}^{5}=1\,,\quad\widetilde{R}\widetilde{T}=\widetilde{T}\widetilde{R}\,.

(82)

The group ID of
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
in
GAP
system is
[600,54]
, and it is isomorphic to the direct product of
A^{\prime}_{5}
and
Z_{5}
, i.e.,
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}\cong A^{\prime}_{5}\times Z_{5}
. Hence it is more convenient to choose another set of generators
S
,
T
and
U
which obey the relations

S^{2}=R,\quad(ST)^{3}=T^{5}=R^{2}=1,\quad U^{5}=1,\quad US=SU,\quad UT=TU\,.

(83)

Notice that the generators
S
and
T
generate a
A^{\prime}_{5}
subgroup, and
U
generates a
Z_{5}
subgroup.
The generators
S
,
T
and
U
are related to
\widetilde{S}
,
\widetilde{T}
as follows

\displaystyle S=\widetilde{R}^{2}\widetilde{S}=\left(\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}\,,~\omega_{5}^{4}(-\tau)^{1/5}\right)\,,

\displaystyle T=\widetilde{R}^{-2}\widetilde{T}=\left(\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\,,\omega^{3}_{5}\right)\,,

\displaystyle U=\widetilde{S}^{12}=\left(\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\,,\omega_{5}\right)\,,

(84)

and vice versa

\widetilde{S}=U^{-2}S,\quad\widetilde{T}=U^{2}T,\quad\widetilde{R}=UR\,.

(85)

The irreducible representations of
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
can be obtained from the tensor products of the irreducible representations of
A^{\prime}_{5}
and
Z^{U}_{5}
where the superscript
U
denotes the generator of the
Z_{5}
subgroup.
The irreducible representations of
A^{\prime}_{5}
are summarized in table
11
, and abelian subgroup
Z^{U}_{5}
has five one-dimensional irreducible representations and the generator
U
is represented by
\omega^{j}_{5}
with
j=0,1,2,3,4
. We shall denote the irreducible representation of
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
as
\mathbf{r}^{j}
which is the tensor product of the
A^{\prime}_{5}
irreducible representation
\mathbf{r}
with the
Z^{U}_{5}
representation
\omega^{j}_{5}
. The generators
S
,
T
and
U
are represented by

\displaystyle\mathbf{r}^{j}~:~\rho_{\mathbf{r}^{j}}(S)=\rho_{\mathbf{r}}(S)\,,\quad\rho_{\mathbf{r}^{j}}(T)=\rho_{\mathbf{r}}(T)\,,\quad\rho_{\mathbf{r}^{j}}(U)=\omega_{5}^{j}\,,

(86)

where
\rho_{\mathbf{r}}
is the representation matrix of the
A^{\prime}_{5}
irreducible representation
\mathbf{r}
as shown in Appendix
A
. The Kronecker products of
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
can be easily obtained from those of
A^{\prime}_{5}
given in Eq. (
A.12
) by including an extra index
j
for each
A^{\prime}_{5}
representation. For instance, we have
\mathbf{2}^{3}\otimes\mathbf{3}^{4}=\mathbf{2}^{2}\oplus\mathbf{4}^{\prime 2}
, and the corresponding Clebsch-Gordan coefficients coefficients remains the same as those of
A^{\prime}_{5}
, which are listed in the Appendix
A
.

It has been shown that the modular forms of weight
k/5
(
k:
non-negative integers) can be constructed for the principal congruence subgroup
\Gamma(5)

[
68
]
, and a multiplier
v_{5}(\gamma)
is necessary so that
v(\gamma)(c\tau+d)^{k/5}
is the correct automorphy factor satisfying the cocycle relation.
The graded ring of modular forms of weight
k/5
at level 5 can be generated by two algebraically independent weight
1/5
modular forms
[
68
]
:

\mathcal{M}(\Gamma(5))=\mathbb{C}[f_{1}(\tau),~f_{2}(\tau)]\,,

(87)

with

f_{1}(\tau)=\dfrac{\theta_{(\frac{1}{10},\frac{1}{2})}(5\tau)}{\eta(\tau)^{3/5}}\,,~~~~f_{2}(\tau)=\dfrac{\theta_{(\frac{3}{10},\frac{1}{2})}(5\tau)}{\eta(\tau)^{3/5}}\,.

(88)

Notice that the theta constant is defined as
[
68
]

\theta_{(m^{\prime},m^{\prime\prime})}(\tau)=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi i[\frac{1}{2}(m+m^{\prime})^{2}\tau+(m+m^{\prime})m^{\prime\prime}]}\,,

(89)

and
\eta(\tau)
is the well-known Dedekind eta function given in Eq. (
B.2
).
Hence the linear space of the modular forms of weight
k/5
and level 5 has dimension
k+1
, and the linear independent basis vectors are
k+1
polynomials of
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
with degree
k
. Under the action of the generators
S
and
T
of the modular group,
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
transform as
[
68
]

\displaystyle f_{1}(\tau)\xrightarrow{S}f_{1}(-\frac{1}{\tau})=\omega_{5}^{2}(-\tau)^{1/5}\frac{1}{\sqrt{5}}\left[\left(\omega_{5}^{3}-\omega_{5}\right)f_{1}(\tau)+\left(\omega_{5}^{2}-\omega_{5}\right)f_{2}(\tau)\right],\,,

\displaystyle f_{2}(\tau)\xrightarrow{S}f_{2}(-\frac{1}{\tau})=\omega_{5}^{2}(-\tau)^{1/5}\frac{1}{\sqrt{5}}\left[\left(\omega_{5}^{3}-\omega_{5}^{2}\right)f_{1}(\tau)+\left(\omega_{5}-\omega_{5}^{3}\right)f_{2}(\tau)\right]\,,

\displaystyle f_{1}(\tau)\xrightarrow{T}f_{1}(\tau+1)=f_{1}(\tau),~~~~f_{2}(\tau)\xrightarrow{T}f_{2}(\tau+1)=\omega_{5}f_{2}(\tau)\,.

(90)

Analogous to the half-integral weight modular forms
[
58
]
, the modular forms of weight
r=k/5
and level 5 can be arranged into different irreducible representations
Y^{(r)}_{\mathbf{r}^{j}}(\tau)
of the finite metaplectic group
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
. The modular multiplet
Y^{(r)}_{\mathbf{r}^{j}}(\tau)
transform under
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}=\widetilde{\Gamma}/\widetilde{\Gamma}(5)
as

Y^{(r)}_{\mathbf{r}^{j}}(\widetilde{\gamma}\tau)=\phi^{5r}(\gamma,\tau)\rho_{\mathbf{r}^{j}}(\widetilde{\gamma})Y^{(r)}_{\mathbf{r}^{j}}(\tau)\,,

(91)

where
\widetilde{\gamma}
stands for a representative element of
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
.
Applying Eq. (
91
) to the element
\widetilde{\gamma}=U
, we find that the constraint
5r+j=0\,(\texttt{mod}~5)
should be fulfilled.
In the group representation basis chosen above, the two weight
1/5
modular forms can be arranged into a
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
doublet
\mathbf{2}^{4}
as follows,

Y^{(\frac{1}{5})}_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}(\tau)\equiv\begin{pmatrix}F_{1}(\tau)\\
F_{2}(\tau)\end{pmatrix},~~~F_{1}(\tau)=e^{-\frac{\pi i}{10}}f_{1}(\tau),~~~F_{2}(\tau)=e^{-\frac{3\pi i}{10}}f_{2}(\tau)\,.

(92)

The
q
-series expressions of
F_{1}(\tau)
and
F_{2}(\tau)
are given by

F_{1}(\tau)\,\eta(\tau)^{3/5}=q^{1/40}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{m}q^{(5m^{2}+m)/2},~~~~F_{2}(\tau)\,\eta(\tau)^{3/5}=q^{9/40}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{m}q^{(5m^{2}+3m)/2}\,,

(93)

with
q=e^{2\pi i\tau}
. Using the transformation rules in Eq. (
90
), we find that

Y^{(\frac{1}{5})}_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}(\tau)
really transforms in the two-dimensional representation
\mathbf{2}^{4}
of
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
,

\displaystyle Y^{(\frac{1}{5})}_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}(\tau)\xrightarrow{S}Y^{(\frac{1}{5})}_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}(-\frac{1}{\tau})=\omega_{5}^{4}(-\tau)^{1/5}\,\rho_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}(S)Y^{(\frac{1}{5})}_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}(\tau)\,,

\displaystyle Y^{(\frac{1}{5})}_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}(\tau)\xrightarrow{T}Y^{(\frac{1}{5})}_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}(\tau+1)=\omega^{3}_{5}\,\rho_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}(T)Y^{(\frac{1}{5})}_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}(\tau)\,,

(94)

and it is invariant under the generator
U
.
Using the Clebsch-Gordan coefficients of
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
, we can construct the higher rational weight modular forms through the tensor products of

Y^{(\frac{1}{5})}_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}(\tau)
. At weight
r=2/5
, we have

\displaystyle Y^{(\frac{2}{5})}_{\mathbf{3}^{3}}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(Y^{(\frac{1}{5})}_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}Y^{(\frac{1}{5})}_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}\right)_{\mathbf{3}^{3}}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{2}F_{1}F_{2}\\
-F_{2}^{2}\\
F_{1}^{2}\end{pmatrix}\,,

(95)

where an overall constant
-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}
is multiplied to make the resulting expression relatively simple. Note that another contraction
\left(Y^{(\frac{1}{5})}_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}Y^{(\frac{1}{5})}_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}\right)_{\mathbf{1}^{3}}=0
because of the antisymmetric Clebsch-Gordan coefficients.
In exactly the same fashion, other rational weight modular forms can be obtained, and we present linearly independent rational weight modular forms up to weight
1
:

\displaystyle r=3/5:~~~~~~~~~Y^{(\frac{3}{5})}_{\mathbf{4}^{\prime 2}}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\left(Y^{(\frac{1}{5})}_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}Y^{(\frac{2}{5})}_{\mathbf{3}^{3}}\right)_{\mathbf{4}^{\prime 2}}=\begin{pmatrix}F_{1}^{3}\\
-\sqrt{3}F_{1}^{2}F_{2}\\
\sqrt{3}F_{1}F_{2}^{2}\\
F_{2}^{3}\\
\end{pmatrix},

(96)

\displaystyle r=4/5:~~~~~~~~~Y^{(\frac{4}{5})}_{\mathbf{5}^{1}}=\frac{1}{2}\left(Y^{(\frac{1}{5})}_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}Y^{(\frac{3}{5})}_{\mathbf{4}^{\prime 2}}\right)_{\mathbf{5}^{1}}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{6}F_{1}^{2}F_{2}^{2}\\
2F_{1}F_{2}^{3}\\
F_{2}^{4}\\
F_{1}^{4}\\
-2F_{1}^{3}F_{2}\end{pmatrix},

(97)

\displaystyle r=1:~~~~~~~~~Y^{(1)}_{\mathbf{6}^{0}}=-\left(Y^{(\frac{1}{5})}_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}Y^{(\frac{4}{5})}_{\mathbf{5}^{1}}\right)_{\mathbf{6}^{0}}=\begin{pmatrix}F_{1}^{5}+2F_{2}^{5}\\
2F_{1}^{5}-F_{2}^{5}\\
5F_{1}^{4}F_{2}\\
5\sqrt{2}F_{1}^{3}F_{2}^{2}\\
-5\sqrt{2}F_{1}^{2}F_{2}^{3}\\
5F_{1}F_{2}^{4}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(98)

From Eqs. (
93
,
B.2
), we can read out the
q
-expansion of the
Y^{(1)}_{\mathbf{6}^{0}}
as follow,

Y^{(1)}_{\mathbf{6}^{0}}(\tau)=\begin{pmatrix}1+5q+10q^{3}-5q^{4}+5q^{5}+\dots\\
2+5q+10q^{2}+5q^{4}+5q^{5}+\dots\\
5q^{1/5}\left(1+2q+2q^{2}+q^{3}+2q^{4}+2q^{5}+\dots\right)\\
5\sqrt{2}q^{2/5}\left(1+q+q^{2}+q^{3}+2q^{4}+q^{6}+\dots\right)\\
-5\sqrt{2}q^{3/5}\left(1+q^{2}+q^{3}+q^{4}-q^{5}+\dots\right)\\
5q^{4/5}\left(1-q+2q^{2}+2q^{6}-2q^{7}+\dots\right)\end{pmatrix}\,,

(99)

which is exactly identical with the
q-
expansion expressions of weight 1 modular form obtained from the Dedekind eta function and Klein forms in Eq. (
B.10
). Furthermore, comparing Eq. (
98
) with Eq. (
B.8
), we find that theta constants and the Klein forms are closely related as follows

\theta_{(\frac{1}{10},\frac{1}{2})}(\tau)=e^{\frac{\pi i}{10}}\eta^{3}(\tau)\mathfrak{k}_{\frac{2}{5},0}(\tau)\,,~~~\theta_{(\frac{3}{10},\frac{1}{2})}(\tau)=e^{\frac{3\pi i}{10}}\eta^{3}(\tau)\mathfrak{k}_{\frac{1}{5},0}(\tau)\,.

(100)

These two identities can be proved by using the famous Jacobi triple product identity as shown
in Appendix
C
.
As a consequence, the basis vectors
e_{1,2,3,4,5,6}(\tau)
of weight 1 modular form at level 5 are
polynomials of degree 5 in
F_{1}(\tau)
and
F_{2}(\tau)
,

\displaystyle e_{1}(\tau)=F_{1}^{5}(\tau)\,,~~~~e_{2}(\tau)=F_{1}^{4}(\tau)F_{2}(\tau)\,,~~~~e_{3}(\tau)=F_{1}^{3}(\tau)F_{2}^{2}(\tau)\,,~~

\displaystyle e_{4}(\tau)=F_{1}^{2}(\tau)F_{2}^{3}(\tau)\,,~~~~e_{5}(\tau)=F_{1}(\tau)F_{2}^{4}(\tau)\,,~~~~e_{6}(\tau)=F_{2}^{5}(\tau)\,.

(101)

Because the two weight
1/5
modular forms
F_{1}(\tau)
and
F_{2}(\tau)
are algebraically independent, the use of
F_{1}(\tau)
and
F_{2}(\tau)
considerably simplifies the process of finding linearly independent modular multiplets, and it is not necessary to examine the constraints relating redundant higher weight multiplets when constructing modular forms in terms of
F_{1}(\tau)
and
F_{2}(\tau)
. For instance, it is easy to check that the constraints Eqs. (
19
,
20
) for the weight 1 modular forms are now trivially fulfilled.

6.1
A lepton model with rational weight modular forms at level 5

All
A^{\prime}_{5}
modular models, in particular the phenomenologically viable models discussed in sections
4
and
5
, can be reproduced from the
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
modular symmetry if the modular weights of both matter fields and modular forms are integral. Obviously the metaplectic covering group
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
has more richer structure of modular forms which can be utilized to explain the flavor structure of lepton and quark in the bottom-up modular invariance approach. In a similar fashion as we have done for the
A^{\prime}_{5}
modular symmetry, one can systematically classify the modular invariant lepton and quark models based on
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
. As an example, we present a benchmark lepton model which involves rational weight modular forms at level 5.

In this model, the neutrino masses arises from the type-I seesaw mechanism. The three generations of left-handed lepton
L
are assumed to transform as a triplet
\mathbf{3}^{3}
under
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
, the right-handed charged leptons
E^{c}_{1,2,3}
are assigned to be
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
singlets
\mathbf{1}^{2}
,
\mathbf{1}^{4}
and
\mathbf{1}^{4}
respectively. Three right-handed neutrinos are introduced, and they transform as a triplet
\mathbf{3^{\prime}}^{0}
. We summarize the modular transformation properties and weights assignment for the lepton and Higgs superfields as follow
4
4

4

Note that in order to ensure the consistency of the theory
[
58
]
, the original modular transformation Eq. (
36
) should be adjusted to metaplectic modular transformation:
\tau\to\widetilde{\gamma}\tau,\quad\Phi_{I}\to\phi^{-5k_{I}}(\gamma,\tau)\rho_{I}(\widetilde{\gamma})\Phi_{I}
, where
\phi(\gamma,\tau)^{5}=c\tau+d
,
\widetilde{\gamma}=(\gamma,\phi(\gamma,\tau))\in\widetilde{\Gamma}
and
-k_{I}
is the modular weight of superfield
\Phi_{I}
.
,

\displaystyle\rho_{L}=\mathbf{3}^{3}\,,\quad\rho_{E^{c}_{1,2,3}}=\mathbf{1}^{2}\oplus\mathbf{1}^{4}\oplus\mathbf{1}^{4}\,,\quad\rho_{N^{c}}=\mathbf{3^{\prime}}^{0}\,,\quad\rho_{H_{u}}=\rho_{H_{d}}=\mathbf{1}^{0}\,,

\displaystyle k_{L}=\frac{3}{5}\,,\quad k_{E^{c}_{1}}=\frac{7}{5}\,,\quad k_{E^{c}_{2}}=-\frac{1}{5}\,,\quad k_{E^{c}_{3}}=\frac{9}{5}\,,\quad k_{N^{c}}=1\,,\quad k_{H_{u}}=k_{H_{d}}=0\,.

(102)

Thus we can read out the following modular invariant superpotential for charged leptons and neutrinos,

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{e}=\alpha\left(Y^{(2)}_{\mathbf{3}^{0}}E_{1}^{c}LH_{d}\right)_{\mathbf{1}^{0}}+\beta\left(Y^{(\frac{2}{5})}_{\mathbf{3}^{3}}E_{2}^{c}LH_{d}\right)_{\mathbf{1}^{0}}+\gamma\left(Y^{(\frac{12}{5})}_{\mathbf{3}^{3}}E_{3}^{c}LH_{d}\right)_{\mathbf{1}^{0}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\nu}=g_{1}\left(Y^{(\frac{8}{5})}_{\mathbf{4}^{2}}N^{c}LH_{u}\right)_{\mathbf{1}^{0}}+g_{2}\left(Y^{(\frac{8}{5})}_{\mathbf{5}^{2}}N^{c}LH_{u}\right)_{\mathbf{1}^{0}}+\Lambda\left(Y^{(2)}_{\mathbf{5}^{0}}N^{c}N^{c}\right)_{\mathbf{1}^{0}}\,.

(103)

Hence the charged lepton and neutrino mass matrices are given by

\displaystyle M_{e}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha Y^{(2)}_{\mathbf{3}^{0},1}~&~\alpha Y^{(2)}_{\mathbf{3}^{0},3}~&~\alpha Y^{(2)}_{\mathbf{3}^{0},2}\\[7.22743pt]
\beta Y^{(\frac{2}{5})}_{\mathbf{3}^{3},1}~&~\beta Y^{(\frac{2}{5})}_{\mathbf{3}^{3},3}~&~\beta Y^{(\frac{2}{5})}_{\mathbf{3}^{3},2}\\[7.22743pt]
\gamma Y^{(\frac{12}{5})}_{\mathbf{3}^{3},1}~&~\gamma Y^{(\frac{12}{5})}_{\mathbf{3}^{3},3}~&~\gamma Y^{(\frac{12}{5})}_{\mathbf{3}^{3},2}\end{pmatrix}v_{d},\quad M_{N}=\Lambda\begin{pmatrix}2Y^{(2)}_{\mathbf{5}^{0},1}~&-\sqrt{3}Y^{(2)}_{\mathbf{5}^{0},4}~&-\sqrt{3}Y^{(2)}_{\mathbf{5}^{0},3}\\[7.22743pt]
-\sqrt{3}Y^{(2)}_{\mathbf{5}^{0},4}~&\sqrt{6}Y^{(2)}_{\mathbf{5}^{0},2}~&-Y^{(2)}_{\mathbf{5}^{0},1}\\[7.22743pt]
-\sqrt{3}Y^{(2)}_{\mathbf{5}^{0},3}~&-Y^{(2)}_{\mathbf{5}^{0},1}~&\sqrt{6}Y^{(2)}_{\mathbf{5}^{0},5}\end{pmatrix}\,,

\displaystyle M_{D}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{3}g_{2}Y^{(\frac{8}{5})}_{\mathbf{5}^{2},1}~&~\sqrt{2}g_{1}Y^{(\frac{8}{5})}_{\mathbf{4}^{2},4}+g_{2}Y^{(\frac{8}{5})}_{\mathbf{5}^{2},5}~&~\sqrt{2}g_{1}Y^{(\frac{8}{5})}_{\mathbf{4}^{2},1}+g_{2}Y^{(\frac{8}{5})}_{\mathbf{5}^{2},2}\\[7.22743pt]
-\sqrt{2}g_{1}Y^{(\frac{8}{5})}_{\mathbf{4}^{2},3}+g_{2}Y^{(\frac{8}{5})}_{\mathbf{5}^{2},4}~&~-g_{1}Y^{(\frac{8}{5})}_{\mathbf{4}^{2},2}-\sqrt{2}g_{2}Y^{(\frac{8}{5})}_{\mathbf{5}^{2},3}~&~g_{1}Y^{(\frac{8}{5})}_{\mathbf{4}^{2},4}-\sqrt{2}g_{2}Y^{(\frac{8}{5})}_{\mathbf{5}^{2},5}\,,\\[7.22743pt]
-\sqrt{2}g_{1}Y^{(\frac{8}{5})}_{\mathbf{4}^{2},2}+g_{2}Y^{(\frac{8}{5})}_{\mathbf{5}^{2},3}~&~g_{1}Y^{(\frac{8}{5})}_{\mathbf{4}^{2},1}-\sqrt{2}g_{2}Y^{(\frac{8}{5})}_{\mathbf{5}^{2},2}~&~-g_{1}Y^{(\frac{8}{5})}_{\mathbf{4}^{2},3}-\sqrt{2}g_{2}Y^{(\frac{8}{5})}_{\mathbf{5}^{2},4}\end{pmatrix}v_{u}\,.

(104)

The parameters
\alpha
,
\beta
,
\gamma
and
g_{1}
can be taken real by redefining the unphysical phases of fields. Thus we have only two complex parameters
\tau
and
g_{2}
left. The charged lepton masses can be reproduced by adjusting the parameters
\alpha
,
\beta
and
\gamma
. The light neutrino mass matrix
m_{\nu}=-M^{T}_{D}M^{-1}_{N}M_{D}
depends on a single complex parameter
g_{2}/g_{1}
and an overall scale
g^{2}_{1}v^{2}_{u}/\Lambda
besides the complex modulus
\tau
. Hence this model effectively depends on 8 free real parameters at low energy. Numerically scanning over the parameter space, we find a good agreement between the model predictions and experimental data
can be achieved for the following values of the input parameters

\begin{gathered}\langle\tau\rangle=-0.16639+1.09859i\,,\qquad\beta/\alpha=0.11310\,,\qquad\gamma/\alpha=0.000268\,,\\
g_{2}/g_{1}=0.17295+0.24924i\,,~~\alpha v_{d}=639.62166~\text{MeV},~~\frac{g_{1}v_{u}^{2}}{\Lambda}=35.71373~\text{meV}\,.\end{gathered}

(105)

Accordingly the predictions for the lepton mixing parameters and neutrino masses are given by

\displaystyle\sin^{2}\theta_{12}=0.30398\,,~~\sin^{2}\theta_{13}=0.02219\,,~~\sin^{2}\theta_{23}=0.57297\,,~~\delta_{CP}=1.4454\pi\,,

\displaystyle\alpha_{21}=0.7135\pi\,,~~\alpha_{31}=1.1178\pi\,,~~~m_{e}/m_{\mu}=0.00473\,,~~~m_{\mu}/m_{\tau}=0.05857\,,

\displaystyle m_{1}=4.3331~\text{meV}\,,~~m_{2}=9.6424~\text{meV}\,,~~m_{3}=50.3562~\text{meV}\,,

\displaystyle m_{\beta}=9.8328~\text{meV}\,,~~~~m_{\beta\beta}=3.5926~\text{meV}\,,

(106)

which are compatible with the experimental data at
1\sigma
level
[
73
]
.
The mass ordering is normal, and the
Planck bound
\sum_{i}m_{i}<120
meV
[
81
]
is fulfilled. The effective masses
m_{\beta}
in beta decay and
m_{\beta\beta}
in neutrinoless double beta decay are very tiny and consequently they are outside the reach of the next generation experiments. We comprehensively scan over the parameter space of this model, and all lepton masses and mixing angles are required to lie in
3\sigma
regions
[
73
]
. Some interesting correlations between the input parameters and observables are shown in figure
2
. It can be seen that the three CP violating phases
\delta_{CP}
,
\alpha_{21}
and
\alpha_{31}
are strongly correlated with each other. Moreover, the allowed range of the lightest neutrino mass
m_{1}
is [3.61meV, 6.75meV] in this model.

Figure 2:
The predicted correlations among the input parameters, neutrino mixing angles and CP violating phases in the lepton model with rational weight modular forms at level 5.

7
Conclusion

The inhomogeneous finite modular groups
\Gamma_{N}
together with even weight modular forms have been extensively studied
in bottom-up flavor model building, while the homogeneous finite modular groups
\Gamma^{\prime}_{N}
are less discussed. Generally
\Gamma_{N}
is isomorphic to the quotient of
\Gamma^{\prime}_{N}
modulo
Z_{2}
subgroup generated by
R=S^{2}
, or we say
\Gamma^{\prime}_{N}
is the double covering of
\Gamma_{N}
. The transformation of the general integral weight modular forms can be described by unitary representations of
\Gamma^{\prime}_{N}
. In the present work, we perform a comprehensive study of the homogeneous finite modular group
\Gamma^{\prime}_{5}\cong A^{\prime}_{5}
at level 5. The vacuum alignment problem of traditional
A^{\prime}_{5}
flavor symmetry models
[
64
,
65
,
66
]

would be simplified considerably, and the flavor symmetry is spontaneously broken by the vacuum expectation value of
\tau
.

The integral weight modular forms of level 5 can be written as a polynomial
\sum_{i=0}^{5k}c_{i}F^{i}_{1}(\tau)F^{5k-i}_{2}(\tau)
, where
F_{1}(\tau)
and
F_{2}(\tau)
are two algebraically independent weight
1/5
modular forms and they are expressed in terms of theta constants and Dedekind eta function. Hence the linear space of modular forms of weight
k
and level 5 has dimension
5k+1
. One can also construct level 5 modular forms with Klein form and Dedekind eta function. We find that both construction methods give the same results by using the identities Eq. (
100
) between theta constants and Klein form. There are six linearly independent lowest weight 1 and level 5 modular forms, and they can be arranged into a six dimensional representation of
A^{\prime}_{5}
. Moreover, we explicitly give the expressions of
A^{\prime}_{5}
multiplets of modular forms up to weight 6.

It is known that the generalized CP symmetry can be consistently combined with modular symmetry, and the complex modulus
\tau
is required to transform as
\tau\rightarrow-\tau^{*}
up to a modular transformation under the action of generalized CP. Accordingly the modular generators
S
and
T
are mapped to their inverse
S^{-1}
and
T^{-1}
respectively under CP, and we find that it is a class-inverting automorphism of
A^{\prime}_{5}
so that the corresponding CP symmetry is indeed physically well defined in the context of
A^{\prime}_{5}
modular symmetry. In our representation basis, both
S
and
T
are represented by symmetric matrices in all irreducible representations of
A^{\prime}_{5}
, therefore the CP transformation for
\tau\rightarrow-\tau^{*}
is the canonical one
X_{\mathbf{r}}=1
. Moreover all CG coefficients are real in our basis, consequently invariance under generalized CP symmetry requires all coupling constants real.
As a consequence, the vacuum expectation value of
\tau
would be the unique source of both modular and CP symmetry breaking if generalized CP symmetry is imposed on the
A^{\prime}_{5}
modular models.

According the representation assignments for the lepton fields, we systematically classify the lepton models based on
A^{\prime}_{5}
modular symmetry. The left-handed lepton doublets are assigned to a triplet of
A^{\prime}_{5}
, the right-handed charged leptons transform as singlets or the direct sum of a doublet and a singlet under
A^{\prime}_{5}
. The neutrino masses are generated by the type I seesaw mechanism, and both scenarios with two and three right-handed neutrinos are considered. We aim to find out all
A^{\prime}_{5}
modular models with minimal number of free parameters in this work. Numerically minimizing the
\chi^{2}
function for each model, we find out 15 phenomenologically viable models with 9 real free parameters and 10 viable models with 10 real free parameters including the real and imaginary parts of
\tau
, and the results of fit are listed in tables
5
,
6
,
7
. For all these 25 models, two more free parameters would be reduced by imposing CP invariance. We find 9 model with 7 free parameters and 10 models with 8 free parameters can accommodate the experimental data in lepton sector, as shown in table
8
and table
9
. Moreover, we use the
A^{\prime}_{5}
modular symmetry to understand the quark masses and CKM mixing matrix, and a quark-lepton unification model is presented.

Furthermore, we extend the framework to include rational weight modular forms at level 5, and the modular group
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
should be extended to its 5-fold metaplectic covering group. Rational weight modular forms of level 5 transform in representations of the finite metaplectic group
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}\cong A^{\prime}_{5}\times Z_{5}
.
We find that the two weight
1/5
modular forms
F_{1}(\tau)
and
F_{2}(\tau)
furnish a doublet
\mathbf{2}^{4}
of
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
with
Y^{(\frac{1}{5})}_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}(\tau)=(F_{1}(\tau),F_{2}(\tau))^{T}
. The ring of modular forms of level 5 is constructed through the tensor products of
Y^{(\frac{1}{5})}_{\mathbf{2}^{4}}(\tau)
,
and remarkably it is unnecessary to consider the constraints among high weight modular forms in this way because
F_{1}(\tau)
and
F_{2}(\tau)
and algebraically independent and they are the minimal set of functions to construct the modular space of level 5. Finally we provide a concrete lepton model based on
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
, the predictions for lepton masses, mixing angles and CP violating phases are studied numerically, and strong correlations between mixing parameters are found.

At lower levels
N=2,3,4
, the homogeneous (inhomogeneous) finite modular groups
\Gamma^{\prime}_{N}
(
\Gamma_{N}
) only have one-dimensional, two-dimensional and three-dimensional irreducible representations.
Nevertheless,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{5}\cong A^{\prime}_{5}
at level 5 have four-dimensional, five-dimensional and six-dimensional irreducible representations besides the singlet, doublet and triplet representations, and the contractions among doublets and triplets can give rise to quartet, quintet and sextet such as
\mathbf{2}\otimes\mathbf{3}=\mathbf{2}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}}
,
\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{3^{\prime}}=\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}}
,
\mathbf{2}\otimes\mathbf{3^{\prime}}=\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{3}=\mathbf{6}
,
\mathbf{3}\otimes\mathbf{3}=\mathbf{1_{s}}\oplus\mathbf{3_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{5_{s}}
,
\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{3^{\prime}}=\mathbf{1_{s}}\oplus\mathbf{3^{\prime}_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{5_{s}}
and
\mathbf{3}\otimes\mathbf{3^{\prime}}=\mathbf{4}\oplus\mathbf{5}
. Hence level 5 opens up new model building possibilities not available for the levels 2, 3, 4, as shown in the paper. The three right-handed charged leptons are usually assigned to be singlets of the finite modular group such that one can tune the couplings of each charged lepton to easily reproduce the hierarchical charged lepton masses. As shown in table
4
, we can see that the right-handed charged leptons prefer to transform as
\mathbf{2}\oplus\mathbf{1}
or
\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{1}
under
A^{\prime}_{5}
in the minimal lepton models which can explain data, this is a notable feature of
A^{\prime}_{5}
modular symmetry with respect to other modular invariant models of levels
N=2,3,4
.

In summary, we have performed a comprehensive and systematical analysis of the homogeneous finite modular groups
\Gamma^{\prime}_{5}\cong A^{\prime}_{5}
and its metaplectic cover
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}\cong A^{\prime}_{5}\times Z_{5}
in the present work. The integral modular forms at level
N=5
are expressed as polynomials of the weight
1/5
modular forms
F_{1}(\tau)
and
F_{2}(\tau)
, this construction naturally bypasses the need to look for constraints
relating redundant higher weight multiplets. The possible lepton and quark models are classified according to the representation assignments of the matter fields under the
A^{\prime}_{5}
modular group. For the first time, we consider the generalized CP symmetry in the context of
A^{\prime}_{5}
. We find out all the minimal
A^{\prime}_{5}
modular models which can explain the experimental data of lepton masses and mixing angles with/without CP symmetries. Furthermore, we construct a complete model for quarks and leptons based on
A^{\prime}_{5}
modular symmetry in which the observed masses and mixing patterns of both quarks and leptons can be reproduced for a common value of modulus
\tau
. Motivated by the fact that some fields with fractional weights are required to be present in construction of string theory
[
79
,
80
,
11
]
, we consider the modular forms of weight
k/5
in the modular invariance approach, and a benchmark lepton model is constructed. The finite metaplectic group
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
has five singlet representations denoted as
\mathbf{1}^{j}
with
j=0,1,2,3,4
, thus it is more convenient to realize the quark and charged lepton mass hierarchies by assigning the right-handed matter fields to singlets of
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
, see the model in section
6.1
.

In the present work, we have taken the approach of bottom-up flavor model building, it will be interesting to see how the modular symmetries
A^{\prime}_{5}
and
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{5}
together with the rational weight modular forms can be naturally derived in the top-down approach from string theory. Because both representations and modular weights of matter fields and modular forms are not subject to any constraint in the current bottom-up approach,
there are many possibilities for model constructions.
We expect this drawback could be overcome from top-down perspective such as the eclectic flavor scheme
[
11
,
12
,
13
,
14
,
15
]
and thus the framework of modular invariance becomes more constrained and predictive.

Appendix A
Group theory of
A^{\prime}_{5}

As shown in
[
53
]
, the double covering of the icosahedral group
A_{5}
can be generated by three generators
S
,
T
and
R
which obey the rules,

S^{2}=R,\quad T^{5}=(ST)^{3}=R^{2}=1,\quad RT=TR\,,

(A.1)

or equivalently

S^{4}=T^{5}=(ST)^{3}=1,\quad S^{2}T=TS^{2}\,.

(A.2)

There are several different presentations for the group
A^{\prime}_{5}
in the literature. In the Shirai basis
[
82
,
64
]
, all group elements can be expressed in terms of the order four generator
U
and the order ten generator
V
which satisfy the following multiplication rules,

U^{2}=V^{5}=R,\quad(V^{2}UV^{3}UV^{-1}UVUV^{-1})^{3}=1~~[\text{or~}((UV^{-1})^{2}U)^{3}=1],\quad R^{2}=1\,.

(A.3)

The generators
S
and
T
can be related to the Shirai generators
U
and
V
by

S=U,\quad T=V^{2},\quad\text{and vice versa }\quad U=S,\quad V=T^{-2}R\,.

(A.4)

In the presentation of Threlfall
[
83
]
,
A^{\prime}_{5}
is also generated by two generators
a
and
b
with

a^{3}=b^{5}=(ab)^{2}=R,\quad R^{2}=1\,,

(A.5)

which are related to the modular generators
S
and
T
by

S=ab,\quad T=b^{-1}R\quad\text{and vice versa}\quad a=ST^{-1},\quad b=TR\,.

(A.6)

We would like to introduce another presentation as simple as Threlfall’s, the two generators
P
and
Q
obey the relations

P^{2}=Q^{5}=(PQ)^{3}=R,\quad R^{2}=1\,,

(A.7)

which are related to
S
and
T
by

S=P,\quad T=QR,\quad\text{and vice versa}\quad P=S,\quad Q=TR\,.

(A.8)

The presentation described in the work of Cummins and Patera
[
84
]
, involves three generators
A_{1,2,3}
satisfying

A_{1}^{3}=A_{2}^{2}=A_{3}^{2}=R,\quad(A_{1}A_{2})^{3}=(A_{1}A_{3})^{2}=(A_{2}A_{3})^{3}=R,\quad R^{2}=1\,,

(A.9)

which are related to the modular generators as follows,

S=A_{2},~T=A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}^{-1},~~\text{and vice versa}~~A_{1}=TST^{-2},~A_{2}=S,~A_{3}=ST^{-2}ST^{2}S\,.

(A.10)

The group
A^{\prime}_{5}
has 120 elements which is twice as many elements as
A_{5}
,
and all the elements can be divided into the 9 conjugacy classes as follows:

\displaystyle 1C_{1}

\displaystyle=\{1\}\,,

\displaystyle 1C_{2}

\displaystyle=\{R\}=(1C_{1})\cdot R\,,

\displaystyle 20C_{3}

\displaystyle=\{ST,TS,S^{3}T^{4},T^{4}S^{3},T^{2}ST^{4},T^{2}S^{3}T^{2},T^{3}ST^{3},T^{3}S^{3}T,T^{4}ST^{2},TS^{3}T^{3},

\displaystyle~~~~~T^{2}ST^{3}S,T^{3}ST^{2}S,ST^{2}ST^{3},ST^{3}ST^{2},S^{3}T^{3}ST,TST^{3}S^{3},S^{3}T^{2}ST^{4},

\displaystyle~~~~~T^{4}ST^{2}S^{3},ST^{2}ST^{2}S,TST^{3}ST\}\,,

\displaystyle 30C_{4}

\displaystyle=\{S,SR,T^{2}ST^{3},T^{2}ST^{3}R,T^{3}ST^{2},T^{3}ST^{2}R,T^{4}ST,T^{4}STR,TST^{4},TST^{4}R,

\displaystyle~~~~~ST^{2}ST,ST^{2}STR,TST^{2}S,TST^{2}SR,ST^{3}ST^{2}S,ST^{3}ST^{2}SR,ST^{2}ST^{3}S,ST^{2}ST^{3}SR,

\displaystyle~~~~~T^{2}ST^{3}ST^{2},T^{2}ST^{3}ST^{2}R,T^{3}ST^{3}ST,T^{3}ST^{3}STR,TST^{3}ST^{3},TST^{3}ST^{3}R,

\displaystyle~~~~~ST^{2}ST^{3}ST,ST^{2}ST^{3}STR,TST^{3}ST^{2}S,TST^{3}ST^{2}SR,T^{2}ST^{3}ST^{2}S,T^{2}ST^{3}ST^{2}SR\}\,,

\displaystyle 12C_{5}

\displaystyle=\{T,T^{4},T^{3}S,ST^{3},S^{3}T^{2},T^{2}S^{3},T^{2}ST,TST^{2},S^{3}TS,TS^{3}T,T^{3}S^{3}T^{4},T^{4}S^{3}T^{3}\}\,,

\displaystyle 12C_{5}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\{T^{2},T^{3},S^{3}T^{2}S,S^{3}T^{3}S,T^{2}ST^{2}S,ST^{2}ST^{2},T^{3}ST^{3}S,ST^{3}ST^{3},T^{2}ST^{3}ST,

\displaystyle~~~~~TST^{3}ST^{2},T^{3}ST^{2}ST^{4},T^{4}ST^{2}ST^{3}\}\,,

\displaystyle 20C_{6}

\displaystyle=\{STR,TSR,S^{3}T^{4}R,T^{4}S^{3}R,T^{2}ST^{4}R,T^{2}S^{3}T^{2}R,T^{3}ST^{3}R,T^{3}S^{3}TR,T^{4}ST^{2}R,

\displaystyle~~~~~TS^{3}T^{3}R,T^{2}ST^{3}SR,T^{3}ST^{2}SR,ST^{2}ST^{3}R,ST^{3}ST^{2}R,S^{3}T^{3}STR,TST^{3}S^{3}R,

\displaystyle~~~~~S^{3}T^{2}ST^{4}R,T^{4}ST^{2}S^{3}R,ST^{2}ST^{2}SR,TST^{3}STR\}=(20C_{3})\cdot R\,,

\displaystyle 12C_{10}

\displaystyle=\{TR,T^{4}R,T^{3}SR,ST^{3}R,S^{3}T^{2}R,T^{2}S^{3}R,T^{2}STR,TST^{2}R,S^{3}TSR,TS^{3}TR,

\displaystyle~~~~~T^{3}S^{3}T^{4}R,T^{4}S^{3}T^{3}R\}=(12C_{5})\cdot R\,,

\displaystyle 12C_{10}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\{T^{2}R,T^{3}R,S^{3}T^{2}SR,S^{3}T^{3}SR,T^{2}ST^{2}SR,ST^{2}ST^{2}R,T^{3}ST^{3}SR,ST^{3}ST^{3}R,

\displaystyle~~~~~T^{2}ST^{3}STR,TST^{3}ST^{2}R,T^{3}ST^{2}ST^{4}R,T^{4}ST^{2}ST^{3}R\}=(12C_{5}^{\prime})\cdot R\,,

(A.11)

where the number in front denotes the number of group elements contained in this class, and the subscript “
n
” of the notation
C_{n}
is the order of the class. The number of conjugacy classes is the same as the inequivalent irreducible representations. In addition to the five inequivalent irreducible representations
\mathbf{1}
,
\mathbf{3}
,
\mathbf{3^{\prime}}
,
\mathbf{4}
,
\mathbf{5}
of the
A_{5}
group,
A^{\prime}_{5}
has four inequivalent representations
\mathbf{2}
,
\mathbf{2^{\prime}}
,
\mathbf{4^{\prime}}
and
\mathbf{6}
.
The explicit forms of the generators
S
,
T
and
R
in each of the irreducible representations are summarized in table
11
. In the single-valued representations
\mathbf{1}
,
\mathbf{3}
,
\mathbf{3^{\prime}}
,
\mathbf{4}
and
\mathbf{5}
, the generator
R
is represented by the identity matrix
I
, and the elements of
A^{\prime}_{5}
are described by the same matrices which represent the elements in
A_{5}
, consequently the group
A^{\prime}_{5}
can not be distinguished from the group
A_{5}
with these representations. The generator
R
is
-I
in the double-valued representations
\mathbf{2}
,
\mathbf{2^{\prime}}
,
\mathbf{4^{\prime}}
and
\mathbf{6}
. Then one can easily read out the character table of the group
A^{\prime}_{5}
shown in table
10
.

From the character table, one can straightforwardly calculate the multiplication rules of the irreducible representations as follows,

\displaystyle\mathbf{2}\otimes\mathbf{2}=\mathbf{1_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{3_{s}}\,,\quad\mathbf{2}\otimes\mathbf{2^{\prime}}=\mathbf{4}\,,\quad\mathbf{2}\otimes\mathbf{3}=\mathbf{2}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}}\,,\quad\mathbf{2}\otimes\mathbf{3^{\prime}}=\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{3}=\mathbf{6}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{2}\otimes\mathbf{4}=\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{6}\,,\quad\mathbf{2}\otimes\mathbf{4^{\prime}}=\mathbf{3}\oplus\mathbf{5}\,,\quad\mathbf{2}\otimes\mathbf{5}=\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{5}=\mathbf{4^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{6}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{2}\otimes\mathbf{6}=\mathbf{3}\otimes\mathbf{4}=\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{4}\oplus\mathbf{5}\,,\quad\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{2^{\prime}}=\mathbf{1_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{3^{\prime}_{s}}\,,\quad\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{3^{\prime}}=\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{4}=\mathbf{2}\oplus\mathbf{6}\,,\quad\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{4^{\prime}}=\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{5}\,,\quad\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{6}=\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{4}=\mathbf{3}\oplus\mathbf{4}\oplus\mathbf{5}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{3}\otimes\mathbf{3}=\mathbf{1_{s}}\oplus\mathbf{3_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{5_{s}}\,,\quad\mathbf{3}\otimes\mathbf{3^{\prime}}=\mathbf{4}\oplus\mathbf{5}\,,\quad\mathbf{3}\otimes\mathbf{4^{\prime}}=\mathbf{2}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{6}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{3}\otimes\mathbf{5}=\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{5}=\mathbf{3}\oplus\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{4}\oplus\mathbf{5}\,,\quad\mathbf{3}\otimes\mathbf{6}=\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{6_{1}}\oplus\mathbf{6_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{3^{\prime}}=\mathbf{1_{s}}\oplus\mathbf{3^{\prime}_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{5_{s}}\,,\quad\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{4^{\prime}}=\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{6}\,,\quad\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{6}=\mathbf{2}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{6_{1}}\oplus\mathbf{6_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{4}\otimes\mathbf{4}=\mathbf{1_{s}}\oplus\mathbf{3_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{3^{\prime}_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{4_{s}}\oplus\mathbf{5_{s}}\,,\quad\mathbf{4}\otimes\mathbf{4^{\prime}}=\mathbf{4^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{6_{1}}\oplus\mathbf{6_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{4}\otimes\mathbf{5}=\mathbf{3}\oplus\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{4}\oplus\mathbf{5_{1}}\oplus\mathbf{5_{2}}\,,\quad\mathbf{4}\otimes\mathbf{6}=\mathbf{2}\oplus\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}_{1}}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}_{2}}\oplus\mathbf{6_{1}}\oplus\mathbf{6_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{4^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{4^{\prime}}=\mathbf{1_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{3_{s}}\oplus\mathbf{3^{\prime}_{s}}\oplus\mathbf{4_{s}}\oplus\mathbf{5_{a}}\,,\quad\mathbf{4^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{5}=\mathbf{2}\oplus\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{6_{1}}\oplus\mathbf{6_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{4^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{6}=\mathbf{3}\oplus\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{4_{1}}\oplus\mathbf{4_{2}}\oplus\mathbf{5_{1}}\oplus\mathbf{5_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{5}\otimes\mathbf{5}=\mathbf{1_{s}}\oplus\mathbf{3_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{3^{\prime}_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{4_{s}}\oplus\mathbf{4_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{5_{1,s}}\oplus\mathbf{5_{2,s}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{5}\otimes\mathbf{6}=\mathbf{2}\oplus\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}_{1}}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}_{2}}\oplus\mathbf{6_{1}}\oplus\mathbf{6_{2}}\oplus\mathbf{6_{3}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{6}\otimes\mathbf{6}=\mathbf{1_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{3_{1,s}}\oplus\mathbf{3_{2,s}}\oplus\mathbf{3^{\prime}_{1,s}}\oplus\mathbf{3^{\prime}_{2,s}}\oplus\mathbf{4_{s}}\oplus\mathbf{4_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{5_{1,s}}\oplus\mathbf{5_{2,a}}\oplus\mathbf{5_{3,a}}\,,

(A.12)

where the subscripts
\mathbf{s}
and
\mathbf{a}
denote symmetric and antisymmetric combinations respectively. For the product decomposition
\mathbf{3}\otimes\mathbf{6}=\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{6_{1}}\oplus\mathbf{6_{2}}
,
\mathbf{6_{1}}
and
\mathbf{6_{2}}
refer to the two sextet representations appearing in the tensor products of
\mathbf{3}
and
\mathbf{6}
, and similar notations are adopted for other tensor products. We now list the Clebsch-Gordan coefficients which are quite useful in model construction. we use
\alpha_{i}
to indicate the elements of the first representation of the product and
\beta_{i}
to indicate those of the second representation.

\mathbf{2}\otimes\mathbf{2}=\mathbf{1_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{3_{s}}

\mathbf{1_{a}}=\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}

\mathbf{3_{s}}=\begin{pmatrix}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\
-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{2}\otimes\mathbf{2^{\prime}}=\mathbf{4}

\mathbf{4}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\
-\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{2}\otimes\mathbf{3}=\mathbf{2}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}}

\mathbf{2}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{4^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}\\
\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{2}\otimes\mathbf{3^{\prime}}=\mathbf{6}

\mathbf{6}=\begin{pmatrix}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
-\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\
-\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}\\
-\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}\\
-\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{2}\otimes\mathbf{4}=\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{6}

\mathbf{2^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{6}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\
-\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{2}\otimes\mathbf{4^{\prime}}=\mathbf{3}\oplus\mathbf{5}

\mathbf{3}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{5}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\
\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
2\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}\\
2\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}\\
\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{2}\otimes\mathbf{5}=\mathbf{4^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{6}

\mathbf{4^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}2\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}+\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{5}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}-2\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{6}=\begin{pmatrix}-2\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}-2\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
2\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{5}-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}\\
\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
-2\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{2}\otimes\mathbf{6}=\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{4}\oplus\mathbf{5}

\mathbf{3^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{6}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{4}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{5}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{6}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{6}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
-\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{5}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}+\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}\\
-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{5}-2\alpha_{1}\beta_{6}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{6}-2\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}+2\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}-2\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{2^{\prime}}=\mathbf{1_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{3^{\prime}_{s}}

\mathbf{1_{a}}=\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}

\mathbf{3^{\prime}_{s}}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{3}=\mathbf{6}

\mathbf{6}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}\\
-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{3^{\prime}}=\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}}

\mathbf{2^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{4^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\
\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\
-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{4}=\mathbf{2}\oplus\mathbf{6}

\mathbf{2}=\begin{pmatrix}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{6}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}\\

\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{4^{\prime}}=\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{5}

\mathbf{3^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{5}=\begin{pmatrix}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
-2\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\
-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}+\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}\\
-2\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{5}=\mathbf{4^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{6}

\mathbf{4^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}\\
-2\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}\\
2\alpha_{2}\beta_{5}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}+\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{6}=\begin{pmatrix}3\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\
3\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}\\
\sqrt{6}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}+2\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}-2\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}\\
2\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{5}\\
\sqrt{6}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}-2\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{6}=\mathbf{3}\oplus\mathbf{4}\oplus\mathbf{5}

\mathbf{3}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{6}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{4}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{5}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{6}\\
-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{5}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{6}\alpha_{1}\beta_{6}-\sqrt{6}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\
3\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}\\
2\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}-2\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{5}\\
2\alpha_{2}\beta_{6}-2\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}-3\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{3}\otimes\mathbf{3}=\mathbf{1_{s}}\oplus\mathbf{3_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{5_{s}}

\mathbf{1_{s}}=\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}

\mathbf{3_{a}}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}-\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}\\
\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{5_{s}}=\begin{pmatrix}2\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\

\sqrt{6}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\
\sqrt{6}\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}\\
-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{3}\otimes\mathbf{3^{\prime}}=\mathbf{4}\oplus\mathbf{5}

\mathbf{4}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}\\

\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{5}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}\\

\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\

\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{3}\otimes\mathbf{4}=\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{4}\oplus\mathbf{5}

\mathbf{3^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}\\

\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}-\alpha_{3}\beta_{4}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{4}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}\\
-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{4}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{5}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{6}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}-\sqrt{6}\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}\\

3\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}-3\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{3}\beta_{4}\\
-2\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}-2\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{3}\otimes\mathbf{4^{\prime}}=\mathbf{2}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{6}

\mathbf{2}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}+\sqrt{3}\alpha_{3}\beta_{4}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{4^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}-3\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}-\sqrt{6}\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}\\
-2\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}-\sqrt{6}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}+\sqrt{6}\alpha_{3}\beta_{4}-2\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\
\sqrt{6}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}+3\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{6}=\begin{pmatrix}2\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}\\
-2\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}\\
\sqrt{6}\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}-2\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}+\sqrt{6}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}\\
\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}+\sqrt{6}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}+\alpha_{3}\beta_{4}\\
\sqrt{6}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}-2\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{3}\otimes\mathbf{5}=\mathbf{3}\oplus\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{4}\oplus\mathbf{5}

\mathbf{3}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{3}\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}+\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{5}-2\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}+\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}-\sqrt{6}\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}\\
\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}+\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}-\sqrt{6}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{3^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{5}\\

\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{5}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{4}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}+2\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}-\sqrt{6}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}\\
2\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}-3\alpha_{3}\beta_{4}\\
3\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}-2\alpha_{3}\beta_{5}\\
\sqrt{6}\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}-2\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{5}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{5}-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}\\

-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}-2\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
2\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{5}\\
\sqrt{3}\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{3}\otimes\mathbf{6}=\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{6_{1}}\oplus\mathbf{6_{2}}

\mathbf{2^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{4}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\
-\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{5}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{6}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{4^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{3}\beta_{4}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}-2\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}-2\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
\sqrt{6}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}+\sqrt{6}\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}+\sqrt{3}\alpha_{3}\beta_{5}\\
\sqrt{6}\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}+\sqrt{6}\alpha_{3}\beta_{6}-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}\\
2\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{5}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}-2\alpha_{1}\beta_{6}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{6_{1}}=\begin{pmatrix}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{6}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{5}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{6}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{6_{2}}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}+\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{6}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{6}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{4}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{6}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{5}-\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{3^{\prime}}=\mathbf{1_{s}}\oplus\mathbf{3^{\prime}_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{5_{s}}

\mathbf{1_{s}}=\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}

\mathbf{3^{\prime}_{a}}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}-\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}\\

\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{5_{s}}=\begin{pmatrix}2\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\

-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\

\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{4}=\mathbf{3}\oplus\mathbf{4}\oplus\mathbf{5}

\mathbf{3}=\begin{pmatrix}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}-\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}\\
\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{4}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{4}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{5}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{6}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}-\sqrt{6}\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}\\

2\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}+2\alpha_{3}\beta_{4}\\
-2\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}-2\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
3\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{4^{\prime}}=\mathbf{2^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{6}

\mathbf{2^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{3}\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}\\
\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{4^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}+\sqrt{6}\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}+2\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}\\

\sqrt{6}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}+3\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
2\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}+\sqrt{6}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{6}=\begin{pmatrix}-\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}-3\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\

\sqrt{6}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}\\

\sqrt{3}\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}+\sqrt{6}\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{5}=\mathbf{3}\oplus\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{4}\oplus\mathbf{5}

\mathbf{3}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{4}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{5}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{3^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{3}\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}+\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}-2\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}+\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}-\sqrt{6}\alpha_{3}\beta_{5}\\
\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}+\sqrt{3}\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}-\sqrt{6}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{4}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}+3\alpha_{2}\beta_{5}-2\alpha_{3}\beta_{4}\\
2\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}+\alpha_{3}\beta_{5}-\sqrt{6}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}\\
\sqrt{6}\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}-2\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
2\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}-3\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{5}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}\\

-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{5}\\
\sqrt{3}\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}+\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}-2\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{3^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{6}=\mathbf{2}\oplus\mathbf{4^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{6_{1}}\oplus\mathbf{6_{2}}

\mathbf{2}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}+\alpha_{3}\beta_{6}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}+\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{4^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{6}\alpha_{3}\beta_{5}-\sqrt{6}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}-\sqrt{3}\alpha_{2}\beta_{6}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{3}\beta_{6}-3\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}-2\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
3\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}-2\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}\\
\sqrt{3}\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}-\sqrt{6}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}-\sqrt{6}\alpha_{1}\beta_{6}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{6_{1}}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{3}\beta_{4}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{5}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}+\alpha_{3}\beta_{5}\\
\alpha_{3}\beta_{6}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}\\
\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{6}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{6_{2}}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}+\alpha_{3}\beta_{4}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{5}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{3}\beta_{4}-\alpha_{2}\beta_{5}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{6}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}\\
\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{5}-\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{6}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{4}\otimes\mathbf{4}=\mathbf{1_{s}}\oplus\mathbf{3_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{3^{\prime}_{a}}\oplus\mathbf{4_{s}}\oplus\mathbf{5_{s}}

\mathbf{1_{s}}=\alpha_{4}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}+\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}

\mathbf{3_{a}}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{4}\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}-\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{4}\beta_{2}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{3^{\prime}_{a}}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{2}\beta_{3}-\alpha_{3}\beta_{2}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{4}-\alpha_{4}\beta_{1}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{3}\beta_{4}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{4}\beta_{3}\\
\sqrt{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}-\sqrt{2}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\mathbf{4_{s}}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{3}\beta_{3}+\alpha_{4}\beta_{2}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{4}\\
\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{4}\beta_{3}+\alpha_{3}\beta_{4}\\
\alpha_{4}\beta_{4}+\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{2}\\
\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}+\alpha_{3}\beta_{1}+\alpha_{1}\beta_{3}\\
